  We present CognitiveCanvas, an interactive tool that transforms conversational AI memories from ephemeral text logs into persistent, manipulable, and audited knowledge objects. \textsc{CognitiveCanvas} integrates: (1) a Hierarchical Semantic Fact-Graph (HSFG) that distills dialogues into Goal, Fact, and Code artifacts, achieving a 92.2\% token reduction while enabling multi-hop GraphRAG traversal; (2) a Continuous Temporal Drift Auditor coupling 60+ regex heuristics with live calendar-anchored LLM verification for 1-click self-correction; and (3) a Spatial-Symbolic Prompt Compiler (SSPC) that translates 2D direct manipulation gestures (Voronoi clustering, node resizing, synthesis edges) directly into structured attention directives

</details>


### [241] [Empirical Insights from AI-Powered Software Engineering in Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23632)
*Tina Mersch,Jack Johns,Steffen Klepke,Hanya Elhashemy,Artur Signell,Robin Gröpler,Klaus Schmid*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: 通过三个案例研究考察生成式AI融入软件工程的实践问题，并给出风险管控与可靠产出建议。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 生成式AI在软件工程中从辅助开发延伸到自主软件工程，应用范围迅速扩大，组织必须应对随之而来的风险与挑战，但缺乏基于实证的整合经验梳理。

Method: 采用案例研究方法，围绕三个案例研究（three case studies）开展实证调查，提炼GenAI集成过程中遇到的实际问题。

Result: 识别出GenAI集成过程中的具体实践问题，并据此提出针对性的风险管理与确保结果可靠性的建议。

Conclusion: 基于实证洞见，为软件工程中有效且负责任的GenAI采用提供指导。

Abstract: Generative Artificial Intelligence (GenAI) is becoming indispensable in future software engineering. Its application spans a wide spectrum - from assisted development to autonomous software engineering, requiring organizations to address associated risks and challenges. This paper investigates these issues through three case studies, highlighting practical problems encountered during GenAI integration. Based on these findings, it provides targeted recommendations for managing risks and ensuring reliable outcomes. This paper provides guidance for effective and responsible GenAI adoption in software engineering based on empirical insights.

</details>


### [242] [Enforcing Code Reasoning in Agentic Scaffold for More Effective Issue Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23782)
*Changshu Liu,Ji young Kim,Alireza Ghazanfari,Saanvi Nallu,Reyhaneh Jabbarvand*

Main category: cs.SE

TL;DR: 该论文分析ReAct式编程智能体的代码推理行为，发现代码推理与更高解决率相关但缺乏引导，提出DeskCheck脚手架系统性引导各阶段代码推理，在SWE-bench-Pro上提升6.29%–10.53%解决率，单实例仅增$0.14成本。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 推理是ReAct式编程智能体的核心，但智能体是否以及如何进行代码推理尚不清晰；且现有推理普遍缺乏引导，导致定位阶段关注表层症状、无法精修接近正确的补丁、忽视issue需求、生成测试的行为多样性不足。

Method: 实证分析：从6种单/多智能体脚手架、4种编程语言、覆盖SWE-bench-Verified/Pro/Lite的7,955条issue解决轨迹中，归纳出12种代码推理形式并考察其与解决率、任务复杂度的关系。方法改进：提出DeskCheck智能体脚手架，在仓库级issue解决全流程中引导代码推理——模拟执行路径定位根因、模拟候选补丁精修近失方案、依据issue需求审计补丁、在生成测试前推理边界输入与预期输出。

Result: 66.95%的轨迹中存在代码推理，其与更高解决率相关且强度随任务复杂度上升。在731个SWE-bench-Pro实例上，DeskCheck相较mini-SWE-Agent和Claude Code提升解决率6.29%–10.53%，额外成本$0.02–$0.28/实例（均值$0.14）。分阶段分析显示方法级定位召回更高、对定位不精确问题的修复更成功、对近正确补丁中遗漏边界情况的检测更好。精确McNemar检验显示所有受试LLM上提升均显著（p<10^-10），重复实验验证对智能体非确定性的稳健性。

Conclusion: 系统性的代码推理是提升编程智能体性能的有效手段，DeskCheck验证了在issue解决各阶段显式引导推理能带来显著且稳健的收益。

Abstract: Reasoning is central to ReAct-based programming agents, yet whether and how they reason about code remains unclear. We study 7,955 issue-resolution trajectories from six single- and multi-agent scaffolds across four programming languages on SWE-bench-Verified, SWE-bench-Pro, and SWE-bench-Lite. We identify 12 forms of code reasoning in 66.95% of trajectories. Code reasoning is associated with higher resolution rates, and its intensity increases with task complexity. However, it remains largely unguided: agents may focus on surface-level symptoms during localization, fail to refine near-correct patches, overlook issue requirements, and generate tests with limited behavioral diversity. To address these limitations, we propose DeskCheck, an agentic scaffold that systematically guides code reasoning throughout repository-level issue resolution. DeskCheck simulates execution paths to localize root causes, simulates candidate patches to refine near-misses, audits patches against issue requirements, and reasons about corner-case inputs and expected outputs before generating tests. On 731 SWE-bench-Pro instances, DeskCheck improves resolution rates over mini-SWE-Agent and Claude Code by 6.29% to 10.53%, at an additional cost of $0.02-$0.28 per instance (average: $0.14). Per-phase analyses show higher method-level localization recall, more successful repairs of imprecisely localized issues, and better detection of missed corner cases in near-correct patches. Exact McNemar tests show statistically significant improvements across all subject LLMs (p < 10^-10), while repeated experiments support robustness to agent nondeterminism. These findings motivate systematic code reasoning as a means of improving programming agents.

</details>
